Well, my daughter drove me here today. We took the highway, but the traffic was just awful near the exit... they're doing construction there again. It seems like they’re always tearing up that same stretch of road every single summer. I was telling one of my regular customers at the store—I've been a cashier over at the Food Mart for years now—that they just need to pave it right the first time instead of patching it. 

But anyway... I don't really know why I need a doctor. I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died. I didn’t call her back when I was supposed to, and... and I had ugly thoughts sometimes. Just awful, ugly thoughts. And I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. It's all my fault. Oh, the church actually had a bake sale last week, but I couldn't bring myself to go. I *am* at fault, though. I know I am. Like I was saying, I didn't call her back when I was supposed to. I had these... these ugly thoughts in my head, and then she was gone. He works over at the electronics store at the mall. He's such a sweet boy, always trying to teach me how to send the little pictures and the smiley faces on it. 

But I didn't use it to call her. And now... well, I just know the Lord is punishing everyone at the church because of the wicked things inside me. It's my fault they're suffering. The roof at the church actually started leaking right over the pews last Sunday. They had to bring out these big orange buckets, the ones you get at the hardware store, just to catch all the water. Well, there *is* a connection, though. I know there is. Because when I'm sitting there on Sunday... well, I sit in the back now, near the vestibule. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I... I don't know if it's just my thoughts doing it, but I know it's my fault. I mean, my thoughts are so ugly, and then things happen. It's like... like when I'm at the store. You know, I've been at the Food Mart so long, I know all the regulars. There's this one gentleman, Mr. Henderson, he comes in every Tuesday for his lottery tickets and a gallon of milk. Always whole milk, never the skim. He says the skim tastes like water, and I have to agree with him there. 

But I don't know about my thoughts making things happen in the world... I just know I'm to blame. God knows what's in my head. He sees it all. And then the church roof leaks, and my friend... she's just gone. I should have called her. I really should have. I was going to, right after I finished making my meatloaf. I have this special recipe, you see, I use a little bit of brown sugar in the ketchup glaze on top. My husband always loved it that way. He used to say it was the best meatloaf in the whole county... but I never made that call.

Like I was saying, I didn't call her back when I was supposed to. I had these... these ugly thoughts in my head, and then she was gone. My phone was sitting right there on the kitchen counter, too. It's one of those new ones my grandson bought me, the kind where you just tap the glass? He works over at the electronics store at the mall. You can always tell when Mary has a cold because she gets this little sniffle right before the high notes. I told her she should take some zinc for it, that's what I always take when I'm standing at the register at the Food Mart and feel a draft coming in through the sliding doors. Those doors are always opening and closing, opening and closing... it's a wonder I don't catch my death of cold every winter.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your thoughts alone, without any action on your part, can cause things to happen in the world around you, like your friend's death or the problems at the church?\n\nPatient: Well, I... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I don't... I don't think anyone is putting thoughts in my head. They're my thoughts. That's the problem, they're so ugly and they're mine. But as for people hearing them... well, I did say the choir and Pastor Thomas know. I don't have to say anything out loud for them to know. God tells them, I suppose. Just like He sees everything. 

It's like when I'm ringing up groceries at the Food Mart. You can just look at people and know things, sometimes. Like Mrs. Gable, she always buys the same brand of cat food, the one in the little blue tins. She must have three or four cats by now. I remember when she just had the one, a little orange tabby. She brought it in a carrier once, right into the store. Mr. Higgins, the manager, he wasn't too happy about that. He's very strict about the health codes. He made her leave it out by the ice machine. That ice machine is always breaking down in the summer, anyway. People come in wanting a bag of ice for their coolers before they head out to the lake, and I have to tell them, 'Sorry, it's out of order again.' It's a real shame, especially on the Fourth of July weekend. We lose a lot of business that way. But no, nobody is putting thoughts in there. They're just my own terrible thoughts.

I just know I'm to blame. God knows what's in my head. He sees it all. And then the church roof leaks, and my friend... she's just gone. I should have called her. I really should have. I was going to, right after I finished making my meatloaf. I have this special recipe, you see, I use a little bit of brown sugar in the ketchup glaze on top. My husband always loved it that way. He used to say it was the best meatloaf in the whole county... but I never made that call.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else or that your thoughts are being heard or known by others without you speaking them out loud?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't... I don't think anyone is putting thoughts in my head. They're my thoughts. That's the problem, they're so ugly and they're mine. But as for people hearing them... well, I did say the choir and Pastor Thomas know. I don't have to say anything out loud for them to know. God tells them, I suppose. Just like He sees everything. \n\nIt's like when I'm ringing up groceries at the Food Mart. You can just look at people and know things, sometimes. Like Mrs. Gable, she always buys the same brand of cat food, the one in the little blue tins. She must have three or four cats by now. I remember when she just had the one, a little orange tabby. She brought it in a carrier once, right into the store. Mr. Higgins, the manager, he wasn't too happy about that. He's very strict about the health codes. He made her leave it out by the ice machine. That ice machine is always breaking down in the summer, anyway. People come in wanting a bag of ice for their coolers before they head out to the lake, and I have to tell them, 'Sorry, it's out of order again.' It's a real shame, especially on the Fourth of July weekend. We lose a lot of business that way. But no, nobody is putting thoughts in there. They're just my own terrible thoughts.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I... I already said nobody is putting thoughts in my head. They're mine. But the broadcasting part... I mean, they don't hear my thoughts like a radio playing out loud, if that's what you mean. But they know. Like I said before, when I'm sitting there on Sunday, and Pastor Thomas is saying the prayers, or the choir sings certain lines... I just know they are really warning me. They know God has marked me, and they're telling everyone that the church is suffering because I brought this on us. 

It's just awful, sitting there knowing they know. I try to wear my nice blue hat, the one with the little silk ribbon, so I can pull the brim down a bit. I bought that hat at Miller's Department Store right before it closed down. It was such a shame when Miller's closed. They had the best hosiery department. Now you have to go all the way out to the big box stores on the highway if you want decent pantyhose, and the girls working there don't know a thing about sizing. I asked one of them for a control top in taupe, and she just stared at me like I was speaking Greek. Kids these days, they just wear those tight yoga pants everywhere anyway. Even to the grocery store! I see them all the time at the Food Mart. But anyway, no, I don't hear my thoughts out loud, they just... they just know what I've done.

You can always tell when Mary has a cold because she gets this little sniffle right before the high notes. I told her she should take some zinc for it, that's what I always take when I'm standing at the register at the Food Mart and feel a draft coming in through the sliding doors. Those doors are always opening and closing, opening and closing... it's a wonder I don't catch my death of cold every winter.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your thoughts alone, without any action on your part, can cause things to happen in the world around you, like your friend's death or the problems at the church?\n\nPatient: Well, I... Always whole milk, never the skim. He says the skim tastes like water, and I have to agree with him there. \n\nBut I don't know about my thoughts making things happen in the world... I don't think anyone is putting thoughts in my head. They're my thoughts. That's the problem, they're so ugly and they're mine. Just like He sees everything. \n\nIt's like when I'm ringing up groceries at the Food Mart. You can just look at people and know things, sometimes. Like Mrs. Gable, she always buys the same brand of cat food, the one in the little blue tins. She must have three or four cats by now. I remember when she just had the one, a little orange tabby. She brought it in a carrier once, right into the store. Mr. Higgins, the manager, he wasn't too happy about that. He's very strict about the health codes. He made her leave it out by the ice machine. That ice machine is always breaking down in the summer, anyway. People come in wanting a bag of ice for their coolers before they head out to the lake, and I have to tell them, 'Sorry, it's out of order again.' It's a real shame, especially on the Fourth of July weekend. We lose a lot of business that way. But no, nobody is putting thoughts in there. They're just my own terrible thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like someone is putting thoughts into your mind that aren’t yours, or that your thoughts are being broadcast out loud for others to hear?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I already said nobody is putting thoughts in my head. They're mine. But the broadcasting part... I mean, they don't hear my thoughts like a radio playing out loud, if that's what you mean. But they know. Like I said before, when I'm sitting there on Sunday, and Pastor Thomas is saying the prayers, or the choir sings certain lines... I just know they are really warning me. They know God has marked me, and they're telling everyone that the church is suffering because I brought this on us. \n\nIt's just awful, sitting there knowing they know. I try to wear my nice blue hat, the one with the little silk ribbon, so I can pull the brim down a bit. I bought that hat at Miller's Department Store right before it closed down. It was such a shame when Miller's closed. They had the best hosiery department. Now you have to go all the way out to the big box stores on the highway if you want decent pantyhose, and the girls working there don't know a thing about sizing. I asked one of them for a control top in taupe, and she just stared at me like I was speaking Greek. Kids these days, they just wear those tight yoga pants everywhere anyway. Even to the grocery store! I see them all the time at the Food Mart. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Doctor, I feel like we're going in circles a bit here. Like I told you, nobody is putting thoughts into my head. They're my thoughts, and they're terrible. And they don't... they don't hear them like a loudspeaker or anything. But they know. 

When I'm sitting in the pew, and Pastor Thomas is saying the prayers, or when the choir sings certain lines... I just know they are really warning me. They're looking right at me, letting me know that God has marked me. They know the church is suffering because I brought this on us. It's so heavy, knowing that. 

It reminds me of how heavy those bags of dog food are at the Food Mart. The big 40-pound ones. We have this one young man, Tommy, who's supposed to help the older folks carry them out to their cars, but half the time he's out back on his phone. I told Mr. Higgins about it, I said, 'Mr. Higgins, Tommy is a sweet boy, but he's got his head in the clouds.' Kids and their phones these days, they just can't put them down. My grandson, little Bobby, he's the same way. He came over for Sunday dinner last month, I made a lovely pot roast with carrots and potatoes, the way my husband used to like it, and Bobby just stared at that little screen the whole time. Didn't even touch the carrots. I always buy the organic ones, too, even though they cost a pretty penny more. But anyway, yes, they just know what I've done, and they use the prayers to warn me.

You can always tell when Mary has a cold because she gets this little sniffle right before the high notes. I told her she should take some zinc for it, that's what I always take when I'm standing at the register at the Food Mart and feel a draft coming in through the sliding doors. Those doors are always opening and closing, opening and closing... it's a wonder I don't catch my death of cold every winter.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your thoughts alone, without any action on your part, can cause things to happen in the world around you, like your friend's death or the problems at the church?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I don't know if it's just my thoughts doing it, but I know it's my fault. I mean, my thoughts are so ugly, and then things happen. It's like... like when I'm at the store. You know, I've been at the Food Mart so long, I know all the regulars. There's this one gentleman, Mr. Henderson, he comes in every Tuesday for his lottery tickets and a gallon of milk. Always whole milk, never the skim. He says the skim tastes like water, and I have to agree with him there. \n\nBut I don't know about my thoughts making things happen in the world... I just know I'm to blame. God knows what's in my head. He sees it all. And then the church roof leaks, and my friend... she's just gone. I should have called her. I really should have. I was going to, right after I finished making my meatloaf. I have this special recipe, you see, I use a little bit of brown sugar in the ketchup glaze on top. My husband always loved it that way. He used to say it was the best meatloaf in the whole county... but I never made that call.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else or that your thoughts are being heard or known by others without you speaking them out loud?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't... I don't think anyone is putting thoughts in my head. They're my thoughts. That's the problem, they're so ugly and they're mine. But as for people hearing them... well, I did say the choir and Pastor Thomas know. I don't have to say anything out loud for them to know. God tells them, I suppose. Just like He sees everything. \n\nIt's like when I'm ringing up groceries at the Food Mart. You can just look at people and know things, sometimes. Like Mrs. Gable, she always buys the same brand of cat food, the one in the little blue tins. She must have three or four cats by now. I remember when she just had the one, a little orange tabby. She brought it in a carrier once, right into the store. Mr. Higgins, the manager, he wasn't too happy about that. He's very strict about the health codes. He made her leave it out by the ice machine. That ice machine is always breaking down in the summer, anyway. People come in wanting a bag of ice for their coolers before they head out to the lake, and I have to tell them, 'Sorry, it's out of order again.' It's a real shame, especially on the Fourth of July weekend. We lose a lot of business that way. But no, nobody is putting thoughts in there. They're just my own terrible thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like someone is putting thoughts into your mind that aren’t yours, or that your thoughts are being broadcast out loud for others to hear?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I already said nobody is putting thoughts in my head. They're mine. But the broadcasting part... I mean, they don't hear my thoughts like a radio playing out loud, if that's what you mean. But they know. Like I said before, when I'm sitting there on Sunday, and Pastor Thomas is saying the prayers, or the choir sings certain lines... I just know they are really warning me. They know God has marked me, and they're telling everyone that the church is suffering because I brought this on us. \n\nIt's just awful, sitting there knowing they know. I try to wear my nice blue hat, the one with the little silk ribbon, so I can pull the brim down a bit. I bought that hat at Miller's Department Store right before it closed down. It was such a shame when Miller's closed. They had the best hosiery department. Now you have to go all the way out to the big box stores on the highway if you want decent pantyhose, and the girls working there don't know a thing about sizing. I asked one of them for a control top in taupe, and she just stared at me like I was speaking Greek. Kids these days, they just wear those tight yoga pants everywhere anyway. Even to the grocery store! I see them all the time at the Food Mart. But anyway, no, I don't hear my thoughts out loud, they just... they just know what I've done.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by an outside force or that your thoughts are being transmitted or heard by others without you speaking them out loud?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I feel like we're going in circles a bit here. Like I told you, nobody is putting thoughts into my head. They're my thoughts, and they're terrible. And they don't... they don't hear them like a loudspeaker or anything. But they know. \n\nWhen I'm sitting in the pew, and Pastor Thomas is saying the prayers, or when the choir sings certain lines... I just know they are really warning me. They're looking right at me, letting me know that God has marked me. They know the church is suffering because I brought this on us. It's so heavy, knowing that. \n\nIt reminds me of how heavy those bags of dog food are at the Food Mart. The big 40-pound ones. We have this one young man, Tommy, who's supposed to help the older folks carry them out to their cars, but half the time he's out back on his phone. I told Mr. Higgins about it, I said, 'Mr. Higgins, Tommy is a sweet boy, but he's got his head in the clouds.' Kids and their phones these days, they just can't put them down. My grandson, little Bobby, he's the same way. He came over for Sunday dinner last month, I made a lovely pot roast with carrots and potatoes, the way my husband used to like it, and Bobby just stared at that little screen the whole time. Didn't even touch the carrots. I always buy the organic ones, too, even though they cost a pretty penny more. But anyway, yes, they just know what I've done, and they use the prayers to warn me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I try to wear my nice blue hat, the one with the little silk ribbon, so I can pull the brim down a bit. I bought that hat at Miller's Department Store right before it closed down. It was such a shame when Miller's closed. They had the best hosiery department. Now you have to go all the way out to the big box stores on the highway if you want decent pantyhose, and the girls working there don't know a thing about sizing. I asked one of them for a control top in taupe, and she just stared at me like I was speaking Greek. Kids these days, they just wear those tight yoga pants everywhere anyway. Even to the grocery store! I see them all the time at the Food Mart. But anyway, no, I don't hear my thoughts out loud, they just... they just know what I've done.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by an outside force or that your thoughts are being transmitted or heard by others without you speaking them out loud?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I feel like we're going in circles a bit here. Like I told you, nobody is putting thoughts into my head. They're my thoughts, and they're terrible. And they don't... they don't hear them like a loudspeaker or anything. But they know. \n\nWhen I'm sitting in the pew, and Pastor Thomas is saying the prayers, or when the choir sings certain lines... I just know they are really warning me. They're looking right at me, letting me know that God has marked me. They know the church is suffering because I brought this on us. It's so heavy, knowing that. \n\nIt reminds me of how heavy those bags of dog food are at the Food Mart. The big 40-pound ones. We have this one young man, Tommy, who's supposed to help the older folks carry them out to their cars, but half the time he's out back on his phone. I told Mr. Higgins about it, I said, 'Mr. Higgins, Tommy is a sweet boy, but he's got his head in the clouds.' Kids and their phones these days, they just can't put them down. My grandson, little Bobby, he's the same way. He came over for Sunday dinner last month, I made a lovely pot roast with carrots and potatoes, the way my husband used to like it, and Bobby just stared at that little screen the whole time. Didn't even touch the carrots. I always buy the organic ones, too, even though they cost a pretty penny more. But anyway, yes, they just know what I've done, and they use the prayers to warn me.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel that things people say or do, like the pastor's prayers or the choir's singing, are specifically directed at you or have special meaning meant just for you?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 74 / cashier / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"When the pastor says the prayers or the choir sings certain lines, I know they are really warning me that God has marked me and that the church is suffering because I brought this on us.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, exactly. I mean, I've been saying that, haven't I? It's like... when I'm sitting there, and Pastor Thomas is reading the prayers, or the choir starts singing certain lines, it's not just part of the service. I know they are looking right at me. They are warning me. They're letting me know that God has marked me, and all the suffering in the church... it's because of me. I brought it on us. It's directed right at me. 

Just like how Mrs. Gable points her finger at me when she wants a specific pack of cigarettes from behind the counter. She smokes those long, thin ones, Virginia Slims, I think. I don't know how she affords it on a fixed income. Cigarettes are so expensive now, almost ten dollars a pack! I remember when my husband, God rest his soul, used to buy a pack for under a dollar. He'd smoke them out on the back porch because I wouldn't let him do it in the house. The smell just clings to the curtains, you know? I had these beautiful lace curtains I ordered from a catalog, took three weeks to arrive. I had to wash them twice a year in the delicate cycle just to keep them white. But anyway, yes, the pastor and the choir... they are definitely directing it all at me. They know I'm the one who brought this on.

We took the highway, but the traffic was just awful near the exit... they're doing construction there again. It seems like they’re always tearing up that same stretch of road every single summer. I was telling one of my regular customers at the store—I've been a cashier over at the Food Mart for years now—that they just need to pave it right the first time instead of patching it. \n\nBut anyway... I don't really know why I need a doctor. I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died. I didn’t call her back when I was supposed to, and... and I had ugly thoughts sometimes. Just awful, ugly thoughts. And I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. It's all my fault. Oh, the church actually had a bake sale last week, but I couldn't bring myself to go. They used to make these little lemon squares that were just wonderful, but the lady who baked them moved down to Florida to live with her sister, so I don't think they have them anymore.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling responsible for your friend's death and that the church is being punished because of you. Have you been feeling guilty or responsible for things that happened even when you weren't really at fault?\n\nPatient: I *am* at fault, though. I know I am. Like I was saying, I didn't call her back when I was supposed to. I had these... these ugly thoughts in my head, and then she was gone. My phone was sitting right there on the kitchen counter, too. It's one of those new ones my grandson bought me, the kind where you just tap the glass? He works over at the electronics store at the mall. He's such a sweet boy, always trying to teach me how to send the little pictures and the smiley faces on it. \n\nBut I didn't use it to call her. The roof at the church actually started leaking right over the pews last Sunday. They had to bring out these big orange buckets, the ones you get at the hardware store, just to catch all the water. Pastor Thomas had to move the whole choir over to the left side of the altar. His robes almost got completely soaked. It's just terrible what I've caused.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your thoughts or actions could actually cause things to happen to other people, like your friend's death or the problems at the church, even if there's no direct connection?\n\nPatient: Well, there *is* a connection, though. I know there is. Because when I'm sitting there on Sunday... well, I sit in the back now, near the vestibule. The wood on those back pews is getting so worn down. My husband, God rest his soul, used to say they needed to sand and revarnish them, but they never did. He was so good with wood, built our whole back porch himself back in '98... \n\nBut when I'm sitting there, and Pastor Thomas says the prayers, or when the choir sings certain lines... I just know they are really warning me. It's not just a regular prayer. They know God has marked me, and they're saying the church is suffering because I brought this on us. \n\nThe choir sounded a bit off last week, anyway. I think Mary—she's the one who sings soprano, she works over at the post office—I think she had a head cold. You can always tell when Mary has a cold because she gets this little sniffle right before the high notes. I told her she should take some zinc for it, that's what I always take when I'm standing at the register at the Food Mart and feel a draft coming in through the sliding doors. Those doors are always opening and closing, opening and closing... it's a wonder I don't catch my death of cold every winter.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your thoughts alone, without any action on your part, can cause things to happen in the world around you, like your friend's death or the problems at the church?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I don't know if it's just my thoughts doing it, but I know it's my fault. I mean, my thoughts are so ugly, and then things happen. It's like... like when I'm at the store. You know, I've been at the Food Mart so long, I know all the regulars. There's this one gentleman, Mr. Henderson, he comes in every Tuesday for his lottery tickets and a gallon of milk. Always whole milk, never the skim. He says the skim tastes like water, and I have to agree with him there. \n\nBut I don't know about my thoughts making things happen in the world... I just know I'm to blame. God knows what's in my head. He sees it all. And then the church roof leaks, and my friend... she's just gone. I should have called her. I really should have. I was going to, right after I finished making my meatloaf. I have this special recipe, you see, I use a little bit of brown sugar in the ketchup glaze on top. My husband always loved it that way. He used to say it was the best meatloaf in the whole county... but I never made that call.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else or that your thoughts are being heard or known by others without you speaking them out loud?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't... I don't think anyone is putting thoughts in my head. They're my thoughts. That's the problem, they're so ugly and they're mine. But as for people hearing them... well, I did say the choir and Pastor Thomas know. I don't have to say anything out loud for them to know. God tells them, I suppose. Just like He sees everything. \n\nIt's like when I'm ringing up groceries at the Food Mart. You can just look at people and know things, sometimes. Like Mrs. Gable, she always buys the same brand of cat food, the one in the little blue tins. She must have three or four cats by now. I remember when she just had the one, a little orange tabby. She brought it in a carrier once, right into the store. Mr. Higgins, the manager, he wasn't too happy about that. He's very strict about the health codes. He made her leave it out by the ice machine. That ice machine is always breaking down in the summer, anyway. People come in wanting a bag of ice for their coolers before they head out to the lake, and I have to tell them, 'Sorry, it's out of order again.' It's a real shame, especially on the Fourth of July weekend. We lose a lot of business that way. But no, nobody is putting thoughts in there. They're just my own terrible thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like someone is putting thoughts into your mind that aren’t yours, or that your thoughts are being broadcast out loud for others to hear?\n\nPatient: Well, I... ========== OUTPUT [doctor] ==========
{
  "candidates": ["F32.3", "F33.3"],
  "note": "The patient's beliefs that events (e.g., friend's death, church roof leaking) are directly tied to her thoughts and actions, and that others (e.g., pastor, choir) are directing messages at her due to her 'wicked' thoughts, suggest delusional guilt or referential thinking. This rules out schizophrenia spectrum disorders (F20.0-F20.9), as there is no clear evidence of thought insertion, thought broadcasting, or third-person hallucinations. The persistent, severe guilt and self-blame, along with possible delusional features, support continuing with F32.3 (Major depressive disorder, severe with psychotic features) and F33.3 (Recurrent depressive disorder, current episode severe with psychotic features).",
  "is_final": false
}

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Doctor, are you having trouble hearing me today? I feel like we keep going over the exact same thing. Yes, even if the rest of the congregation doesn't realize it, I know. They might just sit there and hear a regular hymn, but when the choir sings those specific lines, or when Pastor Thomas says the prayers, it is absolutely meant for me. It is a warning. They are letting me know that God has marked me, and all the suffering happening in our church is my fault because I brought it on us. 

And speaking of the choir, they just got new robes last spring. Deep burgundy ones. They had to replace the old ones because Mildred was in charge of laundering them, and she used way too much bleach. You cannot use bleach on burgundy, it turns it this awful, rusty pink color. I told her she should use Woolite. Woolite is so much gentler on dark fabrics. I use it on my work uniform all the time. I have to wear this dark green polo shirt at the Food Mart, and if you wash it in regular detergent, it looks terribly faded after just a month. They only give you two shirts when you get hired. If you want another one, they take twelve dollars right out of your paycheck! Twelve dollars for a scratchy polyester shirt. It makes me sweat right through it when I have to stand at register four, because that one is right by the hot deli counter. The smell of the fried chicken just gets right into the fabric, and it takes two cycles to get the grease smell out. But yes, the prayers and the singing are definitely meant for me, no matter what anyone else thinks.

I was telling one of my regular customers at the store—I've been a cashier over at the Food Mart for years now—that they just need to pave it right the first time instead of patching it. \n\nBut anyway... I don't really know why I need a doctor. I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died. I didn’t call her back when I was supposed to, and... and I had ugly thoughts sometimes. Just awful, ugly thoughts. And I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. It's all my fault. Oh, the church actually had a bake sale last week, but I couldn't bring myself to go. They used to make these little lemon squares that were just wonderful, but the lady who baked them moved down to Florida to live with her sister, so I don't think they have them anymore.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling responsible for your friend's death and that the church is being punished because of you. Have you been feeling guilty or responsible for things that happened even when you weren't really at fault?\n\nPatient: I *am* at fault, though. I know I am. Like I was saying, I didn't call her back when I was supposed to. I had these... these ugly thoughts in my head, and then she was gone. My phone was sitting right there on the kitchen counter, too. It's one of those new ones my grandson bought me, the kind where you just tap the glass? He works over at the electronics store at the mall. He's such a sweet boy, always trying to teach me how to send the little pictures and the smiley faces on it. \n\nBut I didn't use it to call her. I know there is. Because when I'm sitting there on Sunday... well, I sit in the back now, near the vestibule. The wood on those back pews is getting so worn down. My husband, God rest his soul, used to say they needed to sand and revarnish them, but they never did. I told her she should take some zinc for it, that's what I always take when I'm standing at the register at the Food Mart and feel a draft coming in through the sliding doors. Those doors are always opening and closing, opening and closing... it's a wonder I don't catch my death of cold every winter.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your thoughts alone, without any action on your part, can cause things to happen in the world around you, like your friend's death or the problems at the church?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I don't know if it's just my thoughts doing it, but I know it's my fault. I mean, my thoughts are so ugly, and then things happen. It's like... like when I'm at the store. He used to say it was the best meatloaf in the whole county... but I never made that call.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else or that your thoughts are being heard or known by others without you speaking them out loud?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't... I don't think anyone is putting thoughts in my head. They're my thoughts. That's the problem, they're so ugly and they're mine. Like Mrs. Gable, she always buys the same brand of cat food, the one in the little blue tins. She must have three or four cats by now. I remember when she just had the one, a little orange tabby. She brought it in a carrier once, right into the store. Mr. Higgins, the manager, he wasn't too happy about that. He's very strict about the health codes. He made her leave it out by the ice machine. That ice machine is always breaking down in the summer, anyway. People come in wanting a bag of ice for their coolers before they head out to the lake, and I have to tell them, 'Sorry, it's out of order again.' It's a real shame, especially on the Fourth of July weekend. We lose a lot of business that way. But no, nobody is putting thoughts in there. They're just my own terrible thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like someone is putting thoughts into your mind that aren’t yours, or that your thoughts are being broadcast out loud for others to hear?\n\nPatient: Well, I... They might just sit there and hear a regular hymn, but when the choir sings those specific lines, or when Pastor Thomas says the prayers, it is absolutely meant for me. It is a warning. They are letting me know that God has marked me, and all the suffering happening in our church is my fault because I brought it on us. \n\nAnd speaking of the choir, they just got new robes last spring. Deep burgundy ones. They had to replace the old ones because Mildred was in charge of laundering them, and she used way too much bleach. You cannot use bleach on burgundy, it turns it this awful, rusty pink color. I told her she should use Woolite. Woolite is so much gentler on dark fabrics. I use it on my work uniform all the time. I have to wear this dark green polo shirt at the Food Mart, and if you wash it in regular detergent, it looks terribly faded after just a month. They only give you two shirts when you get hired. If you want another one, they take twelve dollars right out of your paycheck! Twelve dollars for a scratchy polyester shirt. It makes me sweat right through it when I have to stand at register four, because that one is right by the hot deli counter. The smell of the fried chicken just gets right into the fabric, and it takes two cycles to get the grease smell out. But yes, the prayers and the singing are definitely meant for me, no matter what anyone else thinks.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you believe that things people say or do, like the pastor's prayers or the choir's singing, have special meanings or messages that are meant only for you, even if others don't recognize it?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 74 / cashier / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"When the pastor says the prayers or the choir sings certain lines, I know they are really warning me that God has marked me and that the church is suffering because I brought this on us.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Doctor, are you pulling my leg? Or is there something wrong with your notepad? Because I swear I have answered this exact question three times now. Yes. Yes, I do. When Pastor Thomas says the prayers, or the choir sings those specific lines, I know they are warning me. The rest of the people in the pews might just hear a regular service, but I know they are telling me that God has marked me, and that the church is suffering because of what I brought on us. 

It's just like how people don't listen when you tell them something over and over. Like my manager at the Food Mart, Mr. Higgins. I tell him, 'Mr. Higgins, the barcode scanner on register two is sticking again.' You have to press the button three times just to get it to beep. And it slows down my whole line. On Tuesday mornings, it gets so busy because that's when the senior discount applies. Ten percent off if you're over sixty-five. You wouldn't believe how many people come in just to buy canned soup and toilet paper to save a few cents. Mrs. Peterson came in last week and bought twelve cans of cream of mushroom. Twelve! I don't know what she's doing with all that cream of mushroom. Maybe she's making casseroles. I used to make a lovely green bean casserole for Thanksgiving, with those crispy fried onions on top. But I don't really cook much anymore, not since everything started getting so heavy and dark. But yes, the messages in the church, they are just for me. I've told you that.

    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n10\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.3, F33.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, my daughter drove me here today. We took the highway, but the traffic was just awful near the exit... they're doing construction there again. It seems like they’re always tearing up that same stretch of road every single summer. I was telling one of my regular customers at the store—I've been a cashier over at the Food Mart for years now—that they just need to pave it right the first time instead of patching it. \n\nBut anyway... I don't really know why I need a doctor. I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died. I didn’t call her back when I was supposed to, and... and I had ugly thoughts sometimes. Just awful, ugly thoughts. And I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. It's all my fault. Oh, the church actually had a bake sale last week, but I couldn't bring myself to go. They used to make these little lemon squares that were just wonderful, but the lady who baked them moved down to Florida to live with her sister, so I don't think they have them anymore.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling responsible for your friend's death and that the church is being punished because of you. Have you been feeling guilty or responsible for things that happened even when you weren't really at fault?\n\nPatient: I *am* at fault, though. I know I am. Like I was saying, I didn't call her back when I was supposed to. I had these... these ugly thoughts in my head, and then she was gone. My phone was sitting right there on the kitchen counter, too. It's one of those new ones my grandson bought me, the kind where you just tap the glass? He works over at the electronics store at the mall. He's such a sweet boy, always trying to teach me how to send the little pictures and the smiley faces on it. \n\nBut I didn't use it to call her. And now... well, I just know the Lord is punishing everyone at the church because of the wicked things inside me. It's my fault they're suffering. The roof at the church actually started leaking right over the pews last Sunday. They had to bring out these big orange buckets, the ones you get at the hardware store, just to catch all the water. Pastor Thomas had to move the whole choir over to the left side of the altar. His robes almost got completely soaked. It's just terrible what I've caused.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your thoughts or actions could actually cause things to happen to other people, like your friend's death or the problems at the church, even if there's no direct connection?\n\nPatient: Well, there *is* a connection, though. I know there is. Because when I'm sitting there on Sunday... well, I sit in the back now, near the vestibule. The wood on those back pews is getting so worn down. My husband, God rest his soul, used to say they needed to sand and revarnish them, but they never did. He was so good with wood, built our whole back porch himself back in '98... \n\nBut when I'm sitting there, and Pastor Thomas says the prayers, or when the choir sings certain lines... I just know they are really warning me. It's not just a regular prayer. They know God has marked me, and they're saying the church is suffering because I brought this on us. \n\nThe choir sounded a bit off last week, anyway. I think Mary—she's the one who sings soprano, she works over at the post office—I think she had a head cold. You can always tell when Mary has a cold because she gets this little sniffle right before the high notes. I told her she should take some zinc for it, that's what I always take when I'm standing at the register at the Food Mart and feel a draft coming in through the sliding doors. Those doors are always opening and closing, opening and closing... it's a wonder I don't catch my death of cold every winter.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your thoughts alone, without any action on your part, can cause things to happen in the world around you, like your friend's death or the problems at the church?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I don't know if it's just my thoughts doing it, but I know it's my fault. I mean, my thoughts are so ugly, and then things happen. It's like... like when I'm at the store. You know, I've been at the Food Mart so long, I know all the regulars. There's this one gentleman, Mr. Henderson, he comes in every Tuesday for his lottery tickets and a gallon of milk. Always whole milk, never the skim. He says the skim tastes like water, and I have to agree with him there. \n\nBut I don't know about my thoughts making things happen in the world... I just know I'm to blame. God knows what's in my head. He sees it all. And then the church roof leaks, and my friend... she's just gone. I should have called her. I really should have. I was going to, right after I finished making my meatloaf. I have this special recipe, you see, I use a little bit of brown sugar in the ketchup glaze on top. My husband always loved it that way. He used to say it was the best meatloaf in the whole county... but I never made that call.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else or that your thoughts are being heard or known by others without you speaking them out loud?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't... I don't think anyone is putting thoughts in my head. They're my thoughts. That's the problem, they're so ugly and they're mine. But as for people hearing them... well, I did say the choir and Pastor Thomas know. I don't have to say anything out loud for them to know. God tells them, I suppose. Just like He sees everything. \n\nIt's like when I'm ringing up groceries at the Food Mart. You can just look at people and know things, sometimes. Like Mrs. Gable, she always buys the same brand of cat food, the one in the little blue tins. She must have three or four cats by now. I remember when she just had the one, a little orange tabby. She brought it in a carrier once, right into the store. Mr. Higgins, the manager, he wasn't too happy about that. He's very strict about the health codes. He made her leave it out by the ice machine. That ice machine is always breaking down in the summer, anyway. People come in wanting a bag of ice for their coolers before they head out to the lake, and I have to tell them, 'Sorry, it's out of order again.' It's a real shame, especially on the Fourth of July weekend. We lose a lot of business that way. But no, nobody is putting thoughts in there. They're just my own terrible thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like someone is putting thoughts into your mind that aren’t yours, or that your thoughts are being broadcast out loud for others to hear?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I already said nobody is putting thoughts in my head. They're mine. But the broadcasting part... I mean, they don't hear my thoughts like a radio playing out loud, if that's what you mean. But they know. Like I said before, when I'm sitting there on Sunday, and Pastor Thomas is saying the prayers, or the choir sings certain lines... I just know they are really warning me. They know God has marked me, and they're telling everyone that the church is suffering because I brought this on us. \n\nIt's just awful, sitting there knowing they know. I try to wear my nice blue hat, the one with the little silk ribbon, so I can pull the brim down a bit. I bought that hat at Miller's Department Store right before it closed down. It was such a shame when Miller's closed. They had the best hosiery department. Now you have to go all the way out to the big box stores on the highway if you want decent pantyhose, and the girls working there don't know a thing about sizing. I asked one of them for a control top in taupe, and she just stared at me like I was speaking Greek. Kids these days, they just wear those tight yoga pants everywhere anyway. Even to the grocery store! I see them all the time at the Food Mart. But anyway, no, I don't hear my thoughts out loud, they just... they just know what I've done.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by an outside force or that your thoughts are being transmitted or heard by others without you speaking them out loud?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I feel like we're going in circles a bit here. Like I told you, nobody is putting thoughts into my head. They're my thoughts, and they're terrible. And they don't... they don't hear them like a loudspeaker or anything. But they know. \n\nWhen I'm sitting in the pew, and Pastor Thomas is saying the prayers, or when the choir sings certain lines... I just know they are really warning me. They're looking right at me, letting me know that God has marked me. They know the church is suffering because I brought this on us. It's so heavy, knowing that. \n\nIt reminds me of how heavy those bags of dog food are at the Food Mart. The big 40-pound ones. We have this one young man, Tommy, who's supposed to help the older folks carry them out to their cars, but half the time he's out back on his phone. I told Mr. Higgins about it, I said, 'Mr. Higgins, Tommy is a sweet boy, but he's got his head in the clouds.' Kids and their phones these days, they just can't put them down. My grandson, little Bobby, he's the same way. He came over for Sunday dinner last month, I made a lovely pot roast with carrots and potatoes, the way my husband used to like it, and Bobby just stared at that little screen the whole time. Didn't even touch the carrots. I always buy the organic ones, too, even though they cost a pretty penny more. But anyway, yes, they just know what I've done, and they use the prayers to warn me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that things people say or do, like the pastor's prayers or the choir's singing, are specifically directed at you or have special meaning meant just for you?\n\nPatient: Yes, exactly. I mean, I've been saying that, haven't I? It's like... when I'm sitting there, and Pastor Thomas is reading the prayers, or the choir starts singing certain lines, it's not just part of the service. I know they are looking right at me. They are warning me. They're letting me know that God has marked me, and all the suffering in the church... it's because of me. I brought it on us. It's directed right at me. \n\nJust like how Mrs. Gable points her finger at me when she wants a specific pack of cigarettes from behind the counter. She smokes those long, thin ones, Virginia Slims, I think. I don't know how she affords it on a fixed income. Cigarettes are so expensive now, almost ten dollars a pack! I remember when my husband, God rest his soul, used to buy a pack for under a dollar. He'd smoke them out on the back porch because I wouldn't let him do it in the house. The smell just clings to the curtains, you know? I had these beautiful lace curtains I ordered from a catalog, took three weeks to arrive. I had to wash them twice a year in the delicate cycle just to keep them white. But anyway, yes, the pastor and the choir... they are definitely directing it all at me. They know I'm the one who brought this on.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that events or actions happening around you, like the pastor's prayers or the choir singing, are specifically meant for you, even if others don’t see them that way?\n\nPatient: Doctor, are you having trouble hearing me today? You cannot use bleach on burgundy, it turns it this awful, rusty pink color. I told her she should use Woolite. Woolite is so much gentler on dark fabrics. I use it on my work uniform all the time. I have to wear this dark green polo shirt at the Food Mart, and if you wash it in regular detergent, it looks terribly faded after just a month. They only give you two shirts when you get hired. If you want another one, they take twelve dollars right out of your paycheck! Twelve dollars for a scratchy polyester shirt. It makes me sweat right through it when I have to stand at register four, because that one is right by the hot deli counter. The smell of the fried chicken just gets right into the fabric, and it takes two cycles to get the grease smell out. But yes, the prayers and the singing are definitely meant for me, no matter what anyone else thinks.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that things people say or do, like the pastor's prayers or the choir's singing, have special meanings or messages that are meant only for you, even if others don't recognize it?\n\nPatient: Doctor, are you pulling my leg? Doctor, I... I really don't know what to say anymore. Are you feeling alright? Because you've asked me this exact same thing four times now. I feel like I'm talking to a brick wall. Yes, I've told you. When the pastor says the prayers or the choir sings certain lines, I know they are warning me. I know God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on us. 

It's just like trying to explain the lottery machine to old Mr. Henderson. He comes into the Food Mart every Wednesday and Friday, right at 3:15 PM. You can set your watch by him. He always wants three Mega Millions tickets, but he insists on picking his own numbers. And he writes them down on these tiny scraps of paper, like torn off the edges of the newspaper. I have to squint to read his handwriting, and half the time the machine won't accept the numbers because he picks things over 70. I tell him, 'Mr. Henderson, the numbers only go up to 70.' And he just looks at me like I'm speaking Greek. He always wears this yellow windbreaker, no matter what the weather is like. It could be ninety degrees out, and he's wearing that windbreaker. My husband had a windbreaker like that, but his was navy blue. Navy blue is so much more practical, it doesn't show the dirt as much. Especially around the collar. Collars get so grimy if you don't pretreat them with a little bit of Dawn dish soap. Dawn is the best for grease stains. But anyway, yes, the prayers and the songs, they are absolutely meant just for me. I don't know how else to say it.

  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n11\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.3, F33.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, my daughter drove me here today. We took the highway, but the traffic was just awful near the exit... they're doing construction there again. It seems like they’re always tearing up that same stretch of road every single summer. I was telling one of my regular customers at the store—I've been a cashier over at the Food Mart for years now—that they just need to pave it right the first time instead of patching it. \n\nBut anyway... I don't really know why I need a doctor. I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died. I didn’t call her back when I was supposed to, and... and I had ugly thoughts sometimes. Just awful, ugly thoughts. And I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. It's all my fault. Oh, the church actually had a bake sale last week, but I couldn't bring myself to go. They used to make these little lemon squares that were just wonderful, but the lady who baked them moved down to Florida to live with her sister, so I don't think they have them anymore.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling responsible for your friend's death and that the church is being punished because of you. Have you been feeling guilty or responsible for things that happened even when you weren't really at fault?\n\nPatient: I *am* at fault, though. I know I am. Like I was saying, I didn't call her back when I was supposed to. I had these... these ugly thoughts in my head, and then she was gone. My phone was sitting right there on the kitchen counter, too. It's one of those new ones my grandson bought me, the kind where you just tap the glass? He works over at the electronics store at the mall. He's such a sweet boy, always trying to teach me how to send the little pictures and the smiley faces on it. \n\nBut I didn't use it to call her. And now... well, I just know the Lord is punishing everyone at the church because of the wicked things inside me. It's my fault they're suffering. The roof at the church actually started leaking right over the pews last Sunday. They had to bring out these big orange buckets, the ones you get at the hardware store, just to catch all the water. Pastor Thomas had to move the whole choir over to the left side of the altar. His robes almost got completely soaked. It's just terrible what I've caused.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your thoughts or actions could actually cause things to happen to other people, like your friend's death or the problems at the church, even if there's no direct connection?\n\nPatient: Well, there *is* a connection, though. I know there is. Because when I'm sitting there on Sunday... well, I sit in the back now, near the vestibule. The wood on those back pews is getting so worn down. My husband, God rest his soul, used to say they needed to sand and revarnish them, but they never did. He was so good with wood, built our whole back porch himself back in '98... \n\nBut when I'm sitting there, and Pastor Thomas says the prayers, or when the choir sings certain lines... I just know they are really warning me. It's not just a regular prayer. They know God has marked me, and they're saying the church is suffering because I brought this on us. \n\nThe choir sounded a bit off last week, anyway. I think Mary—she's the one who sings soprano, she works over at the post office—I think she had a head cold. You can always tell when Mary has a cold because she gets this little sniffle right before the high notes. I told her she should take some zinc for it, that's what I always take when I'm standing at the register at the Food Mart and feel a draft coming in through the sliding doors. Those doors are always opening and closing, opening and closing... it's a wonder I don't catch my death of cold every winter.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your thoughts alone, without any action on your part, can cause things to happen in the world around you, like your friend's death or the problems at the church?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I don't know if it's just my thoughts doing it, but I know it's my fault. I mean, my thoughts are so ugly, and then things happen. It's like... like when I'm at the store. You know, I've been at the Food Mart so long, I know all the regulars. There's this one gentleman, Mr. Henderson, he comes in every Tuesday for his lottery tickets and a gallon of milk. Always whole milk, never the skim. He says the skim tastes like water, and I have to agree with him there. \n\nBut I don't know about my thoughts making things happen in the world... I just know I'm to blame. God knows what's in my head. He sees it all. And then the church roof leaks, and my friend... she's just gone. I should have called her. I really should have. I was going to, right after I finished making my meatloaf. I have this special recipe, you see, I use a little bit of brown sugar in the ketchup glaze on top. My husband always loved it that way. He used to say it was the best meatloaf in the whole county... but I never made that call.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else or that your thoughts are being heard or known by others without you speaking them out loud?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't... I don't think anyone is putting thoughts in my head. They're my thoughts. That's the problem, they're so ugly and they're mine. But as for people hearing them... well, I did say the choir and Pastor Thomas know. I don't have to say anything out loud for them to know. God tells them, I suppose. Just like He sees everything. \n\nIt's like when I'm ringing up groceries at the Food Mart. You can just look at people and know things, sometimes. Like Mrs. Gable, she always buys the same brand of cat food, the one in the little blue tins. She must have three or four cats by now. I remember when she just had the one, a little orange tabby. She brought it in a carrier once, right into the store. Mr. Higgins, the manager, he wasn't too happy about that. He's very strict about the health codes. He made her leave it out by the ice machine. That ice machine is always breaking down in the summer, anyway. People come in wanting a bag of ice for their coolers before they head out to the lake, and I have to tell them, 'Sorry, it's out of order again.' It's a real shame, especially on the Fourth of July weekend. We lose a lot of business that way. But no, nobody is putting thoughts in there. They're just my own terrible thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like someone is putting thoughts into your mind that aren’t yours, or that your thoughts are being broadcast out loud for others to hear?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I already said nobody is putting thoughts in my head. They're mine. But the broadcasting part... I mean, they don't hear my thoughts like a radio playing out loud, if that's what you mean. But they know. Like I said before, when I'm sitting there on Sunday, and Pastor Thomas is saying the prayers, or the choir sings certain lines... I just know they are really warning me. They know God has marked me, and they're telling everyone that the church is suffering because I brought this on us. \n\nIt's just awful, sitting there knowing they know. I try to wear my nice blue hat, the one with the little silk ribbon, so I can pull the brim down a bit. I bought that hat at Miller's Department Store right before it closed down. It was such a shame when Miller's closed. They had the best hosiery department. Now you have to go all the way out to the big box stores on the highway if you want decent pantyhose, and the girls working there don't know a thing about sizing. I asked one of them for a control top in taupe, and she just stared at me like I was speaking Greek. Kids these days, they just wear those tight yoga pants everywhere anyway. Even to the grocery store! I see them all the time at the Food Mart. But anyway, no, I don't hear my thoughts out loud, they just... they just know what I've done.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by an outside force or that your thoughts are being transmitted or heard by others without you speaking them out loud?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I feel like we're going in circles a bit here. Like I told you, nobody is putting thoughts into my head. They're my thoughts, and they're terrible. And they don't... they don't hear them like a loudspeaker or anything. But they know. \n\nWhen I'm sitting in the pew, and Pastor Thomas is saying the prayers, or when the choir sings certain lines... I just know they are really warning me. They're looking right at me, letting me know that God has marked me. They know the church is suffering because I brought this on us. It's so heavy, knowing that. \n\nIt reminds me of how heavy those bags of dog food are at the Food Mart. The big 40-pound ones. We have this one young man, Tommy, who's supposed to help the older folks carry them out to their cars, but half the time he's out back on his phone. I told Mr. Higgins about it, I said, 'Mr. Higgins, Tommy is a sweet boy, but he's got his head in the clouds.' Kids and their phones these days, they just can't put them down. My grandson, little Bobby, he's the same way. He came over for Sunday dinner last month, I made a lovely pot roast with carrots and potatoes, the way my husband used to like it, and Bobby just stared at that little screen the whole time. Didn't even touch the carrots. I always buy the organic ones, too, even though they cost a pretty penny more. But anyway, yes, they just know what I've done, and they use the prayers to warn me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that things people say or do, like the pastor's prayers or the choir's singing, are specifically directed at you or have special meaning meant just for you?\n\nPatient: Yes, exactly. I mean, I've been saying that, haven't I? It's like... when I'm sitting there, and Pastor Thomas is reading the prayers, or the choir starts singing certain lines, it's not just part of the service. I know they are looking right at me. They are warning me. They're letting me know that God has marked me, and all the suffering in the church... it's because of me. I brought it on us. It's directed right at me. \n\nJust like how Mrs. Gable points her finger at me when she wants a specific pack of cigarettes from behind the counter. She smokes those long, thin ones, Virginia Slims, I think. I don't know how she affords it on a fixed income. Cigarettes are so expensive now, almost ten dollars a pack! I remember when my husband, God rest his soul, used to buy a pack for under a dollar. He'd smoke them out on the back porch because I wouldn't let him do it in the house. The smell just clings to the curtains, you know? I had these beautiful lace curtains I ordered from a catalog, took three weeks to arrive. I had to wash them twice a year in the delicate cycle just to keep them white. But anyway, yes, the pastor and the choir... they are definitely directing it all at me. They know I'm the one who brought this on.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that events or actions happening around you, like the pastor's prayers or the choir singing, are specifically meant for you, even if others don’t see them that way?\n\nPatient: Doctor, are you having trouble hearing me today? I feel like we keep going over the exact same thing. Yes, even if the rest of the congregation doesn't realize it, I know. They might just sit there and hear a regular hymn, but when the choir sings those specific lines, or when Pastor Thomas says the prayers, it is absolutely meant for me. It is a warning. They are letting me know that God has marked me, and all the suffering happening in our church is my fault because I brought it on us. \n\nAnd speaking of the choir, they just got new robes last spring. Deep burgundy ones. They had to replace the old ones because Mildred was in charge of laundering them, and she used way too much bleach. You cannot use bleach on burgundy, it turns it this awful, rusty pink color. I told her she should use Woolite. Woolite is so much gentler on dark fabrics. I use it on my work uniform all the time. I have to wear this dark green polo shirt at the Food Mart, and if you wash it in regular detergent, it looks terribly faded after just a month. They only give you two shirts when you get hired. If you want another one, they take twelve dollars right out of your paycheck! Twelve dollars for a scratchy polyester shirt. It makes me sweat right through it when I have to stand at register four, because that one is right by the hot deli counter. The smell of the fried chicken just gets right into the fabric, and it takes two cycles to get the grease smell out. But yes, the prayers and the singing are definitely meant for me, no matter what anyone else thinks.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that things people say or do, like the pastor's prayers or the choir's singing, have special meanings or messages that are meant only for you, even if others don't recognize it?\n\nPatient: Doctor, are you pulling my leg? Or is there something wrong with your notepad? Because I swear I have answered this exact question three times now. Yes. Yes, I do. When Pastor Thomas says the prayers, or the choir sings those specific lines, I know they are warning me. The rest of the people in the pews might just hear a regular service, but I know they are telling me that God has marked me, and that the church is suffering because of what I brought on us. \n\nIt's just like how people don't listen when you tell them something over and over. Like my manager at the Food Mart, Mr. Higgins. I tell him, 'Mr. Higgins, the barcode scanner on register two is sticking again.' You have to press the button three times just to get it to beep. And it slows down my whole line. On Tuesday mornings, it gets so busy because that's when the senior discount applies. Ten percent off if you're over sixty-five. You wouldn't believe how many people come in just to buy canned soup and toilet paper to save a few cents. Mrs. Peterson came in last week and bought twelve cans of cream of mushroom. Twelve! I don't know what she's doing with all that cream of mushroom. Maybe she's making casseroles. I used to make a lovely green bean casserole for Thanksgiving, with those crispy fried onions on top. But I don't really cook much anymore, not since everything started getting so heavy and dark. But yes, the messages in the church, they are just for me. I've told you that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that things people say or do, like the pastor's prayers or the choir's singing, have special meanings or messages that are meant only for you, even if others don't recognize it?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I... I really don't know what to say anymore. Are you feeling alright? Because you've asked me this exact same thing four times now. I feel like I'm talking to a brick wall. Yes, I've told you. When the pastor says the prayers or the choir sings certain lines, I know they are warning me. I know God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on us. \n\nIt's just like trying to explain the lottery machine to old Mr. Henderson. He comes into the Food Mart every Wednesday and Friday, right at 3:15 PM. You can set your watch by him. He always wants three Mega Millions tickets, but he insists on picking his own numbers. And he writes them down on these tiny scraps of paper, like torn off the edges of the newspaper. I have to squint to read his handwriting, and half the time the machine won't accept the numbers because he picks things over 70. I tell him, 'Mr. Henderson, the numbers only go up to 70.' And he just looks at me like I'm speaking Greek. He always wears this yellow windbreaker, no matter what the weather is like. It could be ninety degrees out, and he's wearing that windbreaker. My husband had a windbreaker like that, but his was navy blue. Navy blue is so much more practical, it doesn't show the dirt as much. Especially around the collar. Collars get so grimy if you don't pretreat them with a little bit of Dawn dish soap. Dawn is the best for grease stains. But anyway, yes, the prayers and the songs, they are absolutely meant just for me. I don't know how else to say it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Doctor, are you... are you playing some kind of joke on an old woman? Because you are asking me the exact same thing again. The radio, the TV, the church... it's all the same question! I don't know if your pen ran out of ink and you couldn't write down my answer the first four times, but yes. Yes! When Pastor Thomas does the prayers, or the choir sings, they are warning me. They are telling me God has marked me and the church is suffering because of me. 

It's like you're not even listening to me. It reminds me of my niece, Sarah. She never listens either. I bought her a beautiful fern for her apartment, a Boston fern. They need a lot of humidity, you know. You can't just stick them near a heating vent and expect them to live. I told her, 'Sarah, you have to mist it twice a week.' Did she listen? No. Two weeks later, she calls me crying because the fronds are all brown and dropping everywhere. It's a mess to clean up, those little dry leaves get right into the carpet fibers. You have to use the hose attachment on the vacuum to really get them out. I have a Hoover, the upright kind. It's heavy, though. Pushing it back and forth makes my shoulder ache, right here in the joint. The doctor gave me some cream for it, smells like wintergreen. It clears your sinuses right up just rubbing it on. But anyway, yes, the messages are for me. I really don't think I can explain it any clearer than that.

I don't really know why I need a doctor. I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died. I didn’t call her back when I was supposed to, and... and I had ugly thoughts sometimes. They used to make these little lemon squares that were just wonderful, but the lady who baked them moved down to Florida to live with her sister, so I don't think they have them anymore.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling responsible for your friend's death and that the church is being punished because of you. He's such a sweet boy, always trying to teach me how to send the little pictures and the smiley faces on it. \n\nBut I didn't use it to call her. Pastor Thomas had to move the whole choir over to the left side of the altar. His robes almost got completely soaked. It's just terrible what I've caused.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your thoughts or actions could actually cause things to happen to other people, like your friend's death or the problems at the church, even if there's no direct connection?\n\nPatient: Well, there *is* a connection, though. I know there is. Because when I'm sitting there on Sunday... well, I sit in the back now, near the vestibule. The wood on those back pews is getting so worn down. My husband, God rest his soul, used to say they needed to sand and revarnish them, but they never did. He was so good with wood, built our whole back porch himself back in '98... \n\nBut when I'm sitting there, and Pastor Thomas says the prayers, or when the choir sings certain lines... They know God has marked me, and they're saying the church is suffering because I brought this on us. \n\nThe choir sounded a bit off last week, anyway. I told her she should take some zinc for it, that's what I always take when I'm standing at the register at the Food Mart and feel a draft coming in through the sliding doors. Those doors are always opening and closing, opening and closing... it's a wonder I don't catch my death of cold every winter.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your thoughts alone, without any action on your part, can cause things to happen in the world around you, like your friend's death or the problems at the church?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I don't know if it's just my thoughts doing it, but I know it's my fault. I mean, my thoughts are so ugly, and then things happen. It's like... like when I'm at the store. Like Mrs. Gable, she always buys the same brand of cat food, the one in the little blue tins. She must have three or four cats by now. I remember when she just had the one, a little orange tabby. She brought it in a carrier once, right into the store. Mr. Higgins, the manager, he wasn't too happy about that. He's very strict about the health codes. He made her leave it out by the ice machine. People come in wanting a bag of ice for their coolers before they head out to the lake, and I have to tell them, 'Sorry, it's out of order again.' They're just my own terrible thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like someone is putting thoughts into your mind that aren’t yours, or that your thoughts are being broadcast out loud for others to hear?\n\nPatient: Well, I...
