It makes me sweat right through it when I have to stand at register four, because that one is right by the hot deli counter. But yes, the prayers and the singing are definitely meant for me, no matter what anyone else thinks.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that things people say or do, like the pastor's prayers or the choir's singing, have special meanings or messages that are meant only for you, even if others don't recognize it?\n\nPatient: Doctor, are you pulling my leg? When Pastor Thomas says the prayers, or the choir sings those specific lines, I know they are warning me. The rest of the people in the pews might just hear a regular service, but I know they are telling me that God has marked me, and that the church is suffering because of what I brought on us. \n\nIt's just like how people don't listen when you tell them something over and over. Like my manager at the Food Mart, Mr. Higgins. I tell him, 'Mr. Higgins, the barcode scanner on register two is sticking again.' You have to press the button three times just to get it to beep. And it slows down my whole line. On Tuesday mornings, it gets so busy because that's when the senior discount applies. Ten percent off if you're over sixty-five. You wouldn't believe how many people come in just to buy canned soup and toilet paper to save a few cents. Mrs. Peterson came in last week and bought twelve cans of cream of mushroom. Twelve! I don't know what she's doing with all that cream of mushroom. Maybe she's making casseroles. I used to make a lovely green bean casserole for Thanksgiving, with those crispy fried onions on top. But I don't really cook much anymore, not since everything started getting so heavy and dark. But yes, the messages in the church, they are just for me. I've told you that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that things people say or do, like the pastor's prayers or the choir's singing, have special meanings or messages that are meant only for you, even if others don't recognize it?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I... I really don't know what to say anymore. Are you feeling alright? Because you've asked me this exact same thing four times now. I feel like I'm talking to a brick wall. Yes, I've told you. When the pastor says the prayers or the choir sings certain lines, I know they are warning me. I know God has marked me, and the church is suffering because I brought this on us. \n\nIt's just like trying to explain the lottery machine to old Mr. Henderson. He comes into the Food Mart every Wednesday and Friday, right at 3:15 PM. You can set your watch by him. He always wants three Mega Millions tickets, but he insists on picking his own numbers. And he writes them down on these tiny scraps of paper, like torn off the edges of the newspaper. I have to squint to read his handwriting, and half the time the machine won't accept the numbers because he picks things over 70. I tell him, 'Mr. Henderson, the numbers only go up to 70.' And he just looks at me like I'm speaking Greek. He always wears this yellow windbreaker, no matter what the weather is like. It could be ninety degrees out, and he's wearing that windbreaker. My husband had a windbreaker like that, but his was navy blue. Navy blue is so much more practical, it doesn't show the dirt as much. Especially around the collar. Collars get so grimy if you don't pretreat them with a little bit of Dawn dish soap. Dawn is the best for grease stains. But anyway, yes, the prayers and the songs, they are absolutely meant just for me. I don't know how else to say it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that things happening around you—like something on TV, a song on the radio, or even strangers’ conversations—have special meanings or messages that are meant only for you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Doctor, are you... are you playing some kind of joke on an old woman? Because you are asking me the exact same thing again. The radio, the TV, the church... it's all the same question! I don't know if your pen ran out of ink and you couldn't write down my answer the first four times, but yes. Yes! When Pastor Thomas does the prayers, or the choir sings, they are warning me. They are telling me God has marked me and the church is suffering because of me. \n\nIt's like you're not even listening to me. It reminds me of my niece, Sarah. She never listens either. I bought her a beautiful fern for her apartment, a Boston fern. They need a lot of humidity, you know. You can't just stick them near a heating vent and expect them to live. I told her, 'Sarah, you have to mist it twice a week.' Did she listen? No. Two weeks later, she calls me crying because the fronds are all brown and dropping everywhere. It's a mess to clean up, those little dry leaves get right into the carpet fibers. You have to use the hose attachment on the vacuum to really get them out. I have a Hoover, the upright kind. It's heavy, though. Pushing it back and forth makes my shoulder ache, right here in the joint. The doctor gave me some cream for it, smells like wintergreen. It clears your sinuses right up just rubbing it on. But anyway, yes, the messages are for me. I really don't think I can explain it any clearer than that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
